<a id="manifestation.sandmanor.entangled_ports_forum"></a>
## Entangled Ports Forum

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.sandmanor.entangled_ports_forum`<br>
**Manifestation:** Entangled Ports Forum<br>
**Primary House:** [Sandmanor](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Teach how independent ports, settlements, makers, scripts, patrons, and larger powers negotiate connection under unequal conditions without dissolving local identity or calling every network peaceful exchange.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Distinct Tyrian, Sidonian, Byblian, Arwadian, Cypriot, and western settlement histories; tribute and diplomatic records; monitored access to timber and harbors; alphabetic transmission; and mobile craft objects with recoverable intermediaries. See the [Phoenicia dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_02/PHOENICIA_NINTH_EIGHTH_CENTURIES.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.sandmanor.phoenicia.city_specific_diplomacy_under_asymmetric_power`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.phoenicia.city_specific_diplomacy_under_asymmetric_power), [`gift.sandmanor.phoenicia.networked_settlement_and_port_relationships`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.phoenicia.networked_settlement_and_port_relationships), [`gift.sandmanor.phoenicia.alphabetic_and_craft_transmission_with_named_intermediaries`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.phoenicia.alphabetic_and_craft_transmission_with_named_intermediaries)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Sandmanor receives city-specific relationship mapping, accountable cultural transmission, multilingual encounter, and network identity. It does not receive a unified Phoenician nation, colonial entitlement, merchant innocence, tribute as consent, or a new Court or recognition authority.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A contemporary Sandmanor coastal study forum with separate port thresholds, braided but labeled route lines, agreement and pressure tables, maker-and-material stations, script adaptation panels, destination-community views, and reversible exhibits in the locked palette; no purple-costume pageant, cedar palace, ship-shaped room, or generic colonial map.<br>
**Institutional role:** A design, diplomacy, provenance, and maritime-history curriculum inside existing Sandmanor library, Minoan, Minorian, and Court-adjacent educational boundaries; it cannot negotiate agreements, judge identity, or authorize settlement.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only city relationship maps, treaty and tribute context, settlement provenance, maker and route tracing, script comparison, curriculum, and future bounded artifact or dialogue references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Store city before umbrella label; distinguish tribute, tax, protection, restriction, trade, migration, settlement, dependency, and violence; expose later transmitted foundation traditions; and name local destination communities rather than mapping empty western space.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Pattern; Prefog; Prefig; Minorians; Minoans; Contest of Improvement; Court and Restitution; see [Sandmanor canon links](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** port relationship cartographer<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** city-specific treaty context, route and settlement provenance, maker networks, script transmission, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for independent city-states, Assyrian interaction, maritime and craft networks, and alphabetic transmission; medium for the exact chronology and political form of individual western foundations and rulers preserved chiefly through later king lists.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://oracc.museum.upenn.edu/saao/aebp/essentials/countries/phoenicians/); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.metmuseum.org/essays/the-phoenicians-1500-300-b-c); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://doi.org/10.1093/oxfordhb/9780190499341.001.0001); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/295/)<br>
**Status:** Reviewed flagship; design remains Working until separately adopted<br>
**Ancestral provenance:** [II:2 — Kings, Prophets, and Assyrian Expansion](ages/age_02_classical_civilization/point_02_kings_prophets_and_assyrian_expansion.md)

<a id="manifestation.sandmanor.treaty_memory_court"></a>
## Treaty Memory Court

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.sandmanor.treaty_memory_court`<br>
**Manifestation:** Treaty Memory Court<br>
**Primary House:** [Sandmanor](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Let residents compare how relationships are framed, translated, witnessed, ranked, revised, breached, and remembered without confusing a recorded agreement with equal consent.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Hittite parity and subordination treaties, multilingual diplomatic letters, envoy and dynastic negotiations, treaty prologues and divine witnesses, and archives preserving regional ritual traditions. See the [Hittite Empire dossier](dossiers/age_01_point_05/HITTITE_EMPIRE.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.sandmanor.hittite_empire.treaty_archives_distinguishing_parity_and_subordination`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.hittite_empire.treaty_archives_distinguishing_parity_and_subordination), [`gift.sandmanor.hittite_empire.multilingual_diplomatic_correspondence`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.hittite_empire.multilingual_diplomatic_correspondence), [`gift.sandmanor.hittite_empire.preservation_of_regional_ritual_traditions`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.hittite_empire.preservation_of_regional_ritual_traditions)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Sandmanor receives source-aware diplomacy, relationship classification, translation, and cultural memory. It does not receive imperial hierarchy, extradition demands, tribute, coerced oaths, dynastic marriage, or a new Court, Proof, recognition, or restitution authority.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A modern planted Sandmanor court with several non-hierarchical entrances, parallel-language panels, sender-recipient and power-position maps, revisable obligation bands, source-tablet images, quiet translation rooms, and visible breach histories; no Hattusa gate replica, royal dais, or costume pageant.<br>
**Institutional role:** A library, diplomacy, and interpretation program adjacent to existing Sandmanor cultural and Court systems; it cannot negotiate, ratify, judge, recognize, or enforce an agreement.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only treaty-type comparison, translation layers, envoy routes, relationship maps, source provenance, curriculum, and future bounded dialogue context.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Label parity and subordination separately; record each manuscript, language, party, political rank, coercive condition, divine sanction, and modern translation; never call the Kadesh treaty the first peace treaty.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Pattern; Prefog; Prefig; Minorians; Minoans; Contest of Improvement; Court and Restitution; see [Sandmanor canon links](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** treaty provenance curator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** agreement comparison, translation layers, relationship maps, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for the treaty and diplomatic archive and multilingual practice; high for varied ritual corpora, but medium for reconstructing performance and public reception from palace and temple deposits.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.metmuseum.org/essays/the-hittites); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/377/); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://www.britishmuseum.org/collection/object/W_1913-1011-22); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://www.hethport.uni-wuerzburg.de/CTH/index.php?l=Vertrag&lang=EN); [Scholarly or collection source 5](https://www.hethport.uni-wuerzburg.de/HFR/edition.php?lang=EN)<br>
**Status:** Reviewed flagship; design remains Working until separately adopted<br>
**Ancestral provenance:** [I:5 — Great Bronze Age Powers](ages/age_01_ancient_civilizations/point_05_great_bronze_age_powers.md)

<a id="manifestation.sandmanor.polis_koinon_forum"></a>
## Polis-and-Koinon Negotiation Forum

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.sandmanor.polis_koinon_forum`<br>
**Manifestation:** Polis-and-Koinon Negotiation Forum<br>
**Primary House:** [Sandmanor](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Let creators compare how local poleis and federal communities negotiated membership, representation, decrees, envoys, honors, benefaction, festivals, teaching, autonomy, and exclusion under royal and Roman pressure.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Continuing Hellenistic civic institutions; varied Achaean, Aetolian and other koina; public decrees, embassies and honors; inter-city festivals, gymnasia and schools; civic vitality through the Achaean War and beyond 146. See the [Hellenistic Greek dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_07/HELLENISTIC_GREEK_CIVIC_AND_FEDERAL_WORLDS_281_146.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.sandmanor.hellenistic_greek.layered_polis_and_federal_civic_identity`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.hellenistic_greek.layered_polis_and_federal_civic_identity), [`gift.sandmanor.hellenistic_greek.decree_embassy_and_benefaction_provenance`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.hellenistic_greek.decree_embassy_and_benefaction_provenance), [`gift.sandmanor.hellenistic_greek.inter_city_festival_and_teaching_networks`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.hellenistic_greek.inter_city_festival_and_teaching_networks)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Sandmanor receives layered civic identity, accountable negotiation, reciprocal-record provenance, and situated cultural networks. It does not receive citizenship exclusion, slavery, elite benefaction, garrison pressure, federal coercion, Roman freedom claims, or cultural prestige as Pattern, consent, recognition, or Court authority.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A planted dark-blue Sandmanor forum with local and federal concentric paths, reversible membership panels, decree-and-response stations, envoy routes, benefaction obligation fields, festival and school exchange maps, and visible exclusion boundaries; no agora replica, colonnaded theme district, generic Greek ornament, costume, or triumphal Corinth tableau.<br>
**Institutional role:** A library, diplomacy, theater, public-history, and civic-education program within existing Law of Pattern, Prefog, Prefig, Minorian, Minoan, Contest of Improvement, Court, and Restitution boundaries; it cannot enroll a citizen, recognize identity, or adjudicate a dispute.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only polis and koinon membership comparison, decree and envoy provenance, honor and obligation mapping, festival and curriculum networks, excluded-voice audit, 146 rupture context, and future bounded diplomacy references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Store city, federal body, eligible member, excluded resident, institution, decree, envoy, benefactor, reciprocal obligation, royal or Roman pressure, festival or school, sponsor, local adaptation, dissent, enforcement, and later revision; never let Athens, Achaea, or one inscription stand for the entire umbrella.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Pattern; Prefog; Prefig; Minorians; Minoans; Contest of Improvement; Court and Restitution; see [Sandmanor canon links](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** civic-federal interpretation facilitator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** membership and exclusion maps, decree comparison, envoy and benefaction provenance, festival networks, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for continued polis institutions, public inscriptions, major federal systems, and the 146 Achaean-Roman rupture; medium for representation and everyday reception across uneven local archives; lower wherever a pan-Hellenistic practice is inferred from one region or elite record.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/7CEEC13A74B2F584530531BF3AC95F4B/9781139000949c03_p52-72_CBO.pdf/the-polis-and-federalism.pdf); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/abs/greek-city-states/hellenistic-and-roman-periods/05454620C25EA38E625B406CC176A3DB); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/federalism-in-greek-antiquity/0041A888BE634E58470C065B29DCEA6A); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/9BAF48F6991FACA3CDCE275C9CD80364/S0075426900087139a.pdf/the-origins-of-the-achaean-war.pdf); [Scholarly or collection source 5](https://www.metmuseum.org/exhibitions/listings/2016/pergamon/exhibition-galleries)<br>
**Status:** Reviewed flagship; design remains Working until separately adopted<br>
**Ancestral provenance:** [II:7 — Mature Hellenistic World](ages/age_02_classical_civilization/point_07_mature_hellenistic_world.md)

<a id="manifestation.sandmanor.many_shore_provenance_forum"></a>
## Many-Shore Provenance Forum

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.sandmanor.many_shore_provenance_forum`<br>
**Manifestation:** Many-Shore Provenance Forum<br>
**Primary House:** [Sandmanor](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Make Saite-era contact accountable by tracing each port community, sanctuary, maker, interpreter, soldier, patron, source form, object route, and later excavation history without reducing Egypt to a gateway for outsiders.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Saite Nile and temple institutions, Naukratis as an Egyptian multi-community harbor town, Egyptian and non-Egyptian sanctuaries and dedications, Greek and Carian service, Mediterranean exchange, and selective Saite artistic citation. See the [Saite Egypt dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_04/SAITE_EGYPT_LATE_DYNASTY_26.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.sandmanor.saite_egypt.multi_community_port_and_sanctuary_provenance`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.saite_egypt.multi_community_port_and_sanctuary_provenance), [`gift.sandmanor.saite_egypt.military_and_diplomatic_intermediary_networks`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.saite_egypt.military_and_diplomatic_intermediary_networks), [`gift.sandmanor.saite_egypt.selective_revival_with_present_authorship`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.saite_egypt.selective_revival_with_present_authorship)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Sandmanor receives named intermediary networks, multi-community place memory, object and sanctuary provenance, and authored historical citation. It does not receive pharaonic identity judgment, colonial access, mercenary romance, cultural purity, copied ancient style, or a new Court power.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A planted dark-blue Sandmanor forum organized around a modern water-edge model, with community-specific route tables, sanctuary and object biographies, maker and interpreter credits, reversible design-citation panels, and excavation-dispersal maps; no pseudo-Egyptian harbor, Greek-colony set, crown pageant, or ethnic costume.<br>
**Institutional role:** A library, design, diplomatic-history, and cultural-provenance curriculum inside existing Prefog, Prefig, Minorian, Minoan, Court-adjacent, and restitution boundaries; it cannot judge identity, authorize ceremony, or retroactively transfer ownership.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only port-community maps, object biography and dispersed-collection provenance, interpreter and service networks, sanctuary relationships, design citation, curriculum, and future bounded museum or diplomacy references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Name Egyptian, Greek, Cypriot, Phoenician, Carian, and other actors only when sources support them; distinguish resident, trader, craft worker, soldier, visitor, and later excavator; attach source form, maker, patron, receiving context, and uncertainty to every design citation.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Pattern; Prefog; Prefig; Minorians; Minoans; Contest of Improvement; Court and Restitution; see [Sandmanor canon links](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** cross-shore provenance mediator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** community maps, sanctuary and object biographies, interpreter networks, design citation, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for Saite chronology, Naukratis's multi-community Egyptian setting, harbor and sanctuary evidence, and selective artistic citation; medium for the legal status of communities and service personnel and for individual object routes; low for treating later Herodotean arrangements as a complete contemporary charter.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.britishmuseum.org/research/projects/naukratis-greeks-egypt); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.britishmuseum.org/research/projects/harbour-naukratis); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://www.metmuseum.org/zh/essays/egypt-in-the-late-period-ca-712-332-b-c); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://resources.metmuseum.org/resources/metpublications/pdf/Reliefs_from_the_Tomb_of_the_Vizier_Nespakashuty_The_Metropolitan_Museum_Journal_v_33_1998.pdf)<br>
**Status:** Reviewed flagship; design remains Working until separately adopted<br>
**Ancestral provenance:** [II:4 — Neo-Babylonian World](ages/age_02_classical_civilization/point_04_neo_babylonian_world.md)

## Citation and Identity Atelier

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.sandmanor.citation_and_identity_atelier`<br>
**Manifestation:** Citation and Identity Atelier<br>
**Primary House:** [Sandmanor](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Teach how Kushite and Saite actors selected inherited forms, combined regional identities, named legitimacy, used intermediaries, and altered predecessors' images under changing power.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Kushite rule rooted in Napata and Egyptian Amun institutions, distinct Nubian regalia within pharaonic representation, Twenty-Fifth- and Twenty-Sixth-Dynasty artistic citation, God's Wife networks, Assyrian encounters, and Psamtik I's Saite consolidation. See the [Egypt, Twenty-Fifth and Twenty-Sixth Dynasties dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_03/EGYPT_DYNASTIES_25_26_TRANSITION.md).<br>

**Modern House interpretation:** Sandmanor receives accountable citation, layered public identity, cross-regional ritual interpretation, and intermediary-aware diplomacy. It does not receive racial typology, dynastic purity, pharaonic sovereignty, mercenary romanticism, erased Kushite agency, or a new Court or recognition power.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A contemporary planted Sandmanor atelier with paired object views, source-monument indexes, removable regalia and title layers shown only as records, maker and patron labels, alteration histories, regional route screens, and reversible design studies in the locked palette; no pyramid, temple, crown pageant, or pseudo-Egyptian costume.<br>
**Institutional role:** A design, diplomacy, art-history, and identity curriculum inside existing Sandmanor library, Minoan, Minorian, and Court-adjacent educational boundaries; it cannot judge identity or authorize ceremony.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only visual-source comparison, identity and title layers, object alteration history, intermediary maps, curriculum, and future bounded design-provenance references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Name Kush and Egypt rather than collapsing them; store dynasty, ruler, region, workshop, source form, new context, and later alteration; distinguish artistic citation from political restoration and Assyrian appointment claims from uniform control.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Pattern; Prefog; Prefig; Minorians; Minoans; Contest of Improvement; Court and Restitution; see [Sandmanor canon links](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** citation provenance curator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** visual-source comparison, identity-layer mapping, intermediary records, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for Kushite Egyptian rule, distinctive Kushite regalia, Assyrian invasions, Saite consolidation, and selective archaism; medium for the exact political function of individual artistic citations and intermediaries.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/abs/cambridge-ancient-history/egypt-the-twentyfifth-and-twentysixth-dynasties/EE157154FB2C4A528C279F6D1572BEEF); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://resources.metmuseum.org/resources/metpublications/pdf/Reliefs_from_the_Tomb_of_the_Vizier_Nespakashuty_The_Metropolitan_Museum_Journal_v_33_1998.pdf); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://smarthistory.org/kingdom-kush-nubia-intro/); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://egymonuments.gov.eg/en/historical-periods/late-period/)<br>

## Pastoral Correspondence and Difference Forum

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.sandmanor.pastoral_correspondence_difference_forum`<br>
**Manifestation:** Pastoral Correspondence and Difference Forum<br>
**Primary House:** [Sandmanor](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Compare correspondence, hospitality, care obligations, texts, translations, local offices, disagreement, persecution evidence, confiscation, and restoration claims without staging doctrinal victory.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Diverse third-century Christian communities, pastoral networks, textual debate, Dura-Europos, persecution and restoration records, and the 311/313 legal transition. See the [Early Church dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_11/EARLY_CHURCH_NETWORKS_DEBATE_AND_PERSECUTION_180_313.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.sandmanor.early_church.pastoral_networks`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.early_church.pastoral_networks), [`gift.sandmanor.early_church.communal_identity`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.early_church.communal_identity), [`gift.sandmanor.early_church.theological_debate`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.early_church.theological_debate)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Sandmanor receives relational care, correspondence, translation, and disagreement practice, not doctrine, conversion, martyr prestige, hierarchy, or later unity as Law of Pattern.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A non-devotional forum with correspondence routes, parallel texts, language and custody marks, local-community panes, care ledgers, disagreement tables, restitution records, and source-distance warnings; no church replica or doctrinal program.<br>
**Institutional role:** A cultural-memory, care, diplomacy, and comparative-reading interface under Law of Pattern, consent, Court and Restitution, and bounded Proof.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only letter routing, care handoff, variant comparison, local-community difference, property-restoration provenance, source-confidence display, and curriculum references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Keep city, household, teacher, bishop, council, and later historian distinct; date persecution locally; separate 311 toleration from 313 restoration; do not infer uniform canon or hierarchy.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Pattern; Prefog; Prefig; Minorians; Minoans; Contest of Improvement; Court and Restitution; see [Sandmanor canon links](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** correspondence and difference curator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** correspondence route, language, text variant, care handoff, community difference, confiscation, restitution, and source-confidence references<br>
**Confidence:** High for named writers, major persecution measures, Dura-Europos, and 311/313 texts; variable for community scale, office, property, and later unity claims.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/abs/cambridge-ancient-history/thirdcentury-christianity/3C73C681040134F8E3D4EB681B295CD2); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://resources.metmuseum.org/resources/metpublications/pdf/The_World_between_Empires_A_Picture_Album.pdf); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu/source/edict-milan.asp)<br>

<a id="manifestation.sandmanor.successor_diplomacy_forum"></a>
## Successor Khanate Diplomacy Forum

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.sandmanor.successor_diplomacy_forum`<br>
**Manifestation:** Successor Khanate Diplomacy Forum<br>
**Primary House:** [Sandmanor](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Map successor courts, routes, messengers, kinship, rivalry, tribute, and local memory without restoring a unified empire.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Golden Horde, Chagatai, Ilkhanid, and Yuan successor diplomacy, relay logistics, multilingual envoys, and contested Mongol legacies. See the [Mongol Khanates dossier](dossiers/age_03_point_06/MONGOL_KHANATES_SUCCESSOR_DIPLOMACY_AND_LOGISTICS_1279_1368.md).<br>

**Institutional role:** A cultural-memory, library, theater, and diplomacy interface under existing Sandmanor boundaries.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Route provenance, messenger latency, party and mandate, treaty, refusal, civilian risk, and reception.<br>

**Possible NPC role:** successor-diplomacy curator<br>

**Confidence:** High for major successor chronology and route systems; variable for administrative uniformity and local experience.<br>

<a id="manifestation.sandmanor.humanist_workshop_commons"></a>
## Humanist Workshop Commons

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.sandmanor.humanist_workshop_commons`<br>
**Manifestation:** Humanist Workshop Commons<br>
**Primary House:** [Sandmanor](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Join civic texts, patronage, workshop collaboration, material custody, urban sites, and public reception without reducing plural Italian cities to Florence or to great men.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Italian civic humanism, guild and workshop practice, booksellers and manuscript production, patronage, perspective, architecture, and urban public works. See the [Renaissance Italy dossier](dossiers/age_03_point_07/RENAISSANCE_ITALY_HUMANISM_WORKSHOPS_AND_CIVIC_PATRONAGE_1368_1453.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.sandmanor.renaissance_italy.civic_humanism`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.renaissance_italy.civic_humanism), [`gift.sandmanor.renaissance_italy.workshop_and_patronage_systems`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.renaissance_italy.workshop_and_patronage_systems), [`gift.sandmanor.renaissance_italy.urban_design`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.sandmanor.renaissance_italy.urban_design)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Sandmanor receives design and relationship provenance, not oligarchic authority, patronal ownership of culture, or an aesthetic mandate.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A planted working commons with text tables, commission ledgers, maker and assistant credits, material racks, site models, audience notes, and revision walls; no copied palazzo.<br>
**Institutional role:** A library, design, cultural-memory, education, and public-space interface under existing Sandmanor boundaries.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Text provenance, commission and patron mapping, maker attribution, workshop handoff, site review, dispute, exclusion, and reception.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Separate Florence, Venice, Milan, Siena, Ferrara, Rome, republic, court, guild, church, household, workshop, and labor across 1368–1453.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Pattern; Prefog; Prefig; Minorians; Minoans; Contest of Improvement; Court and Restitution; see [Sandmanor canon links](houses/SANDMANOR_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** humanist-workshop provenance curator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** text, patronage, maker, commission, workshop, site, audience, dispute, and reception references<br>
**Confidence:** High for major works, workshops, and elite civic records; variable for informal labor, women, poorer residents, and regional access.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.metmuseum.org/essays/manuscript-illumination-in-italy-1400-1600); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://82nd-and-fifth.metmuseum.org/toah/ht/08/eustc.html)<br>
**Status:** Reviewed flagship; design remains Working until separately adopted<br>
**Ancestral provenance:** [III:7 — Late Medieval World](ages/age_03_medieval_civilization/point_07_late_medieval_world.md)

# Glaüshouse

<a id="manifestation.glaushouse.living_water_conservatory"></a>
## Living Water Conservatory

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.glaushouse.living_water_conservatory`<br>
**Manifestation:** Living Water Conservatory<br>
**Primary House:** [Glaüshouse](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Join water-quality stewardship, botanical recovery, public learning, and infrastructure maintenance as one visible practice of living-system integrity.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Early Harappan settlement organization, long-lived water-management traditions, and emerging craft and measurement standards. See the [Early Indus Civilization dossier](dossiers/age_01_point_01/EARLY_INDUS_CIVILIZATION.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.glaushouse.early_indus_civilization.planned_settlement_organization`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.early_indus_civilization.planned_settlement_organization), [`gift.glaushouse.early_indus_civilization.water_management_across_long_lived_settlements`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.early_indus_civilization.water_management_across_long_lived_settlements), [`gift.glaushouse.early_indus_civilization.emerging_craft_and_measurement_standardization`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.early_indus_civilization.emerging_craft_and_measurement_standardization)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Glaüshouse reads water infrastructure as preventive care while explicitly separating early evidence from later Mature Harappan urban achievement.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A contemporary glass, ceramic, dark-blue, and living-plant complex with accessible channels, sampling points, replaceable filters, exposed maintenance paths, and quiet recovery rooms; it copies neither the Great Bath nor a Harappan street.<br>
**Institutional role:** A public educational and conservation program within existing Glaüshouse care, ecology, library, or Recovery Ward authority; it creates no Clearance.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only water-condition display, maintenance provenance, ecology curriculum, accessibility route, and future restoration or sampling references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Tag early and mature evidence separately; do not call all drainage public sanitation; preserve consent and clinical-data boundaries.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Integrity; Precog; Prima Donna; Persephones; Nightingales; Chromacord; Recovery Ward; see [Glaüshouse canon links](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** water-systems conservator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** water-quality curriculum, maintenance provenance, and recovery-route reference<br>
**Confidence:** Medium-high for the cumulative water and planning inheritance; medium for assigning specific sophisticated systems to the early phase.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.harappa.com/indus4/e2.html); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/1645/); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/FDCED53A490D2DCCA3DD4CB0B37DECC0/S0003598X00068150a.pdf/rita-p-wright-the-ancient-indus-urbanism-economy-and-society-x396-pages-78-illustrations-13-tables-2010-cambridge-cambridge-university-press-978-0-521-57219-4-hardback-dollar85-and-pound45-978-0-521-5.pdf)<br>

<a id="manifestation.glaushouse.long_horizon_observation_institute"></a>
## Long-Horizon Observation Institute

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.glaushouse.long_horizon_observation_institute`<br>
**Manifestation:** Long-Horizon Observation Institute<br>
**Primary House:** [Glaüshouse](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Preserve long observation series together with archive custody, service conditions, prediction methods, missing intervals, urban and environmental dependencies, and repair history across political transition.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Babylonian astronomical diaries and scholarly tablets, Eanna and Ebabbar temple-community archives, writing boards and administrative records, and royal and archaeological evidence for canals, embankments, temples, walls, and city maintenance. See the [Neo-Babylonian Empire dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_04/NEO_BABYLONIAN_EMPIRE_612_539.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.glaushouse.neo_babylonian_empire.longitudinal_celestial_observation`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.neo_babylonian_empire.longitudinal_celestial_observation), [`gift.glaushouse.neo_babylonian_empire.temple_city_service_archives`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.neo_babylonian_empire.temple_city_service_archives), [`gift.glaushouse.neo_babylonian_empire.canal_and_urban_maintenance_provenance`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.neo_babylonian_empire.canal_and_urban_maintenance_provenance)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Glaüshouse receives longitudinal observation, evidence correction, service-archive custody, and maintenance provenance. It does not receive celestial omen as diagnosis, temple authority as Clearance, imperial extraction as care, or royal rebuilding as proof of Integrity.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A modern dark-blue, glass, ceramic, brick, and planted research environment with time-aligned observation tables, prediction-versus-observation panes, lacuna markers, archive-custody paths, water and service-state diagrams, repair layers, and visible labor-status records; no Ishtar Gate, ziggurat, horoscope chamber, or royal cylinder décor.<br>
**Institutional role:** A research, library, ecology, conservation, and maintenance-learning service within existing Precog, Nightingale, Chromacord, consent, Integrity, and Recovery Ward boundaries; it cannot diagnose, clear, command labor, or establish doctrine.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only observation-series comparison, prediction revision, environmental and service-state correlation, archive custody, maintenance provenance, curriculum, and future bounded research or restoration references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Distinguish observation, omen interpretation, prediction, and later mathematical model; record date, place, copy, observer or corpus, missing interval, and institutional attribution; connect urban works to actual service and labor evidence; carry institutional continuity through 539 without treating political change as no change.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Integrity; Precog; Prima Donna; Persephones; Nightingales; Chromacord; Recovery Ward; see [Glaüshouse canon links](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** long-horizon evidence conservator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** observation-series comparison, archive custody, service-state history, maintenance provenance, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for extensive Neo-Babylonian temple and legal archives, major urban works, and a continuous Babylonian celestial-observation tradition; medium for individual diary dates and archival custody; contested for the exact institutional home of early astronomical diaries and the reach of royal maintenance claims.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://academic.oup.com/edited-volume/27992/chapter-abstract/211698375); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/heavenly-writing/scribes-and-scholars-of-mesopotamian-celestial-science/83475389E396F18CC16632EF2EE0466B); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://oracc.museum.upenn.edu/ribo/babylon7/pager); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://academic.oup.com/book/45753/chapter-abstract/398233098)<br>

<a id="manifestation.glaushouse.bilingual_witness_clinic"></a>
## Bilingual Witness Clinic

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.glaushouse.bilingual_witness_clinic`<br>
**Manifestation:** Bilingual Witness Clinic<br>
**Primary House:** [Glaüshouse](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Preserve each local inscription, language version, treaty relationship, object state, polity, annexation history, copy, translation, and uncertainty while supporting comparison across a distributed regional record.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** The Tell Fekheriye bilingual, Tel Dan fragments, Zakkur and Sefire stelae, Sam'al inscriptions, Assyrian accounts of locally distinct polities, and the continuity of Aramaic language witnesses across uneven conquest and displacement. See the [Aram dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_02/ARAM_POLITIES_NINTH_EIGHTH_CENTURIES.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.glaushouse.aram.local_polity_inscription_and_bilingual_witness`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.aram.local_polity_inscription_and_bilingual_witness), [`gift.glaushouse.aram.distributed_treaty_and_boundary_memory`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.aram.distributed_treaty_and_boundary_memory), [`gift.glaushouse.aram.language_continuity_across_annexation_and_displacement`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.aram.language_continuity_across_annexation_and_displacement)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Glaüshouse receives parallel witness preservation, local variation, treaty and boundary memory, language continuity, and displacement-aware custody. It does not receive one Aram state, a uniform ethnicity, diagnosis by language, later imperial Aramaic backdated, or clinical Clearance from translation.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A modern distributed set of dark-blue, glass, stone, ceramic, and planted reading rooms with paired-text tables, fragment-fit displays, local-polity maps, treaty-party paths, version-difference illumination, custody and displacement tracks, and visible lacunae; no Damascus palace replica, generic caravan imagery, or invented pan-Aramaean ornament.<br>
**Institutional role:** A library, conservation, translation, and access service within existing Precog, Nightingale, consent, custody, Integrity, and Recovery Ward boundaries; it cannot classify a Being or validate an ancient oath as present authority.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only parallel-text alignment, damaged-text reconstruction states, polity and treaty relationship maps, copy and translation lineage, displacement-aware language history, curriculum, and future bounded conservation references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Record local polity, language, genre, speaker, date, findspot, damage, reconstruction, translation, and external label before `Aram`; keep Tell Fekheriye, Tel Dan, Zakkur, Sefire, and Sam'al at their debated dates; separate language continuity from political survival.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Integrity; Precog; Prima Donna; Persephones; Nightingales; Chromacord; Recovery Ward; see [Glaüshouse canon links](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** bilingual witness conservator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** parallel-text alignment, polity and treaty mapping, custody chains, displacement-aware language history, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for the distinct local corpora and substantial ninth–eighth-century Old Aramaic record; medium for precise dates and political readings of individual monuments; low for any model that assigns all regional material culture or language change to one Aramaean identity or one cause.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://doi.org/10.2307/j.ctt1g69w78); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.degruyterbrill.com/document/doi/10.18574/nyu/9781479834648.003.0021/html); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://oracc.museum.upenn.edu/riao/ria5/ShalmaneserIII/); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://www.degruyterbrill.com/serial/sari-b/html); [Scholarly or collection source 5](https://oracc.museum.upenn.edu/rinap/rinap1/)<br>
**Status:** Reviewed flagship; design remains Working until separately adopted<br>

<a id="manifestation.glaushouse.regime_continuity_conservatory"></a>
## Regime-Continuity Conservatory

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.glaushouse.regime_continuity_conservatory`<br>
**Manifestation:** Regime-Continuity Conservatory<br>
**Primary House:** [Glaüshouse](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Preserve how people, services, records, languages, temples, buildings, and obligations persist, fail, or change across Saite, first Achaemenid, restored Egyptian, and renewed Achaemenid rule.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Late Egyptian temple and local institutions; Demotic, hieroglyphic, hieratic and Aramaic records; the Udjahorresnet self-presentation; Memphis and Elephantine evidence; first Persian rule from 525–404, independent Dynasties 28–30, and renewed Persian rule from 343. See the [Late Egypt dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_05/LATE_EGYPT_539_336.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.glaushouse.late_egypt.institutional_continuity_across_changing_sovereignty`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.late_egypt.institutional_continuity_across_changing_sovereignty), [`gift.glaushouse.late_egypt.multilingual_legal_and_administrative_custody`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.late_egypt.multilingual_legal_and_administrative_custody), [`gift.glaushouse.late_egypt.restoration_and_temple_service_provenance`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.late_egypt.restoration_and_temple_service_provenance)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Glaüshouse receives multi-regime service continuity, multilingual custody, restoration provenance, and honest interruption history. It does not receive foreign or native kingship as Integrity, temple office as Clearance, royal restoration as care, collaboration as simple consent, or political independence as automatic health.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A modern dark-blue, glass, ceramic, papyrus-toned, and planted conservatory with parallel sovereignty and service timelines, language-specific custody shelves, damaged-and-restored object stations, local-institution paths, water and building service diagrams, and unresolved-harm fields; no Egyptian temple replica, pharaonic costume, Persian court set, mummy display, or sacred-animal theme.<br>
**Institutional role:** A conservation, library, translation, recovery-research, and public-memory service within existing Law of Integrity, Precog, Nightingale, Chromacord, consent, custody, and Recovery Ward boundaries; it cannot diagnose, clear, establish doctrine, or legitimize rule.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only institution-state timelines, multilingual document custody, restoration claim comparison, service interruption and recovery, archive curriculum, and future bounded conservation references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Represent 525, 404, and 343 separately; attach each text to language, community, legal forum, find context, speaker, and custody; separate royal claim from local service outcome; distinguish continuation, adaptation, interruption, collaboration, revolt, and restoration; carry the second Persian phase to 332 beyond the Point endpoint.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Integrity; Precog; Prima Donna; Persephones; Nightingales; Chromacord; Recovery Ward; see [Glaüshouse canon links](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** regime-continuity conservator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** institution-state timelines, multilingual custody, restoration provenance, service recovery, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for the major regime boundaries and the continued use of several written traditions; high for specific Memphis, Elephantine, Udjahorresnet, and Dynasty 30 evidence; medium for institutional continuity at individual sites; lower for the poorly documented second Persian period and generalized national effects.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.metmuseum.org/zh/essays/egypt-in-the-late-period-ca-712-332-b-c); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.ucl.ac.uk/museums-static/digitalegypt/lp/persian1.html); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://www.ucl.ac.uk/museums-static/digitalegypt/lp/latedynastic.html); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://www.museivaticani.va/content/museivaticani/en/collezioni/musei/museo-gregoriano-egizio/sala-i--reperti-epigrafici/_naoforo-vaticano.html); [Scholarly or collection source 5](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/comparative-studies-in-society-and-history/article/empires-languages-and-scripts-in-the-persoindian-world/FF8D806A9AE63CF6469126FBF3823F18)<br>

**Purpose:** Preserve how Babylon's people, records, temples, services, and scholarly practices persisted or changed through contested regimes, destruction, displacement, rebuilding, civil war, and renewed local kingship.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Babylonian chronicles and legal archives, Sennacherib's 689 destruction claims, Esarhaddon's Babylon rebuilding inscriptions, city and temple continuity, scholarly witnesses, Shamash-shuma-ukin's revolt, and Nabopolassar's transition. See the [Babylon dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_03/BABYLON_CONTESTED_CITY_AND_RESTORATION.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.glaushouse.babylon.city_temple_continuity_across_regime_change`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.babylon.city_temple_continuity_across_regime_change), [`gift.glaushouse.babylon.destruction_and_rebuilding_provenance`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.babylon.destruction_and_rebuilding_provenance), [`gift.glaushouse.babylon.chronicle_and_scholarly_witness_comparison`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.babylon.chronicle_and_scholarly_witness_comparison)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Glaüshouse receives longitudinal custody, damage-and-repair provenance, institutional continuity analysis, and plural scholarly witness. It does not receive divine punishment as diagnosis, royal rebuilding as absolution, temple authority as Clearance, or political survival as living integrity.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A modern dark-blue, glass, brick, ceramic, and planted conservation setting with damage maps, surviving-fragment tables, service-state timelines, reconstruction layers, displaced-custody paths, parallel chronicles, and repaired but visibly joined surfaces; no Ishtar Gate replica, ziggurat, royal brick slogan, or ruin spectacle.<br>
**Institutional role:** A conservation, library, recovery-research, and public-memory service within existing Precog, Nightingale, Chromacord, consent, custody, Integrity, and Recovery Ward boundaries.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only damage-state comparison, restoration provenance, institutional and archive continuity, source conflict, curriculum, and future bounded conservation or recovery references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Record destroyer, affected people and services, surviving fabric, displaced custody, sponsor, labor status, reconstruction claim, material intervention, restored function, unresolved harm, and later reuse; never let rebuilding erase responsibility.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Integrity; Precog; Prima Donna; Persephones; Nightingales; Chromacord; Recovery Ward; see [Glaüshouse canon links](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** restoration evidence conservator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** damage-state comparison, reconstruction provenance, archive continuity, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High for the major regime sequence, 689 destruction, Esarhaddon's rebuilding program, 648 revolt ending, and Nabopolassar's rise; medium for archaeological damage attribution, archive survival paths, and the reach of specific restorations.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://oracc.museum.upenn.edu/saao/aebp/essentials/countries/babylonia/); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://oracc.museum.upenn.edu/rinap/rinap4/Q003335.18.1); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://oracc.museum.upenn.edu/rinap/downloads/0RINAP5_3_final.pdf); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://oracc.museum.upenn.edu/saao/knpp/)<br>

## Multilingual Collection Conservatory

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.glaushouse.multilingual_collection_conservatory`<br>
**Manifestation:** Multilingual Collection Conservatory<br>
**Primary House:** [Glaüshouse](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Keep language, source community, acquisition, custody, translation, access, service history, conservation state, and uncertainty attached to early Ptolemaic records and scholarly collections.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Greek administrative writing beside continuing Demotic and Egyptian scripts; Egyptian temples, scribes, settlements and Nile services under a new court; the Satrap Stela; Alexandria's early court and port; and the uncertain Ptolemy I/Ptolemy II formation of the Museum and Library. See the [Ptolemaic dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_06/EARLY_PTOLEMAIC_FORMATION_332_281.md).<br>

**Modern House interpretation:** Glaüshouse receives multilingual custody, institutional handoff, service continuity, and source-critical collection practice. It does not receive royal acquisition, temple office, administrative language hierarchy, forced transfer, collection scale, or scholarly prestige as Integrity, consent, or Clearance.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A modern dark-blue, glass, ceramic, papyrus-toned, and planted conservatory with parallel script and custody paths, prior-owner and acquisition fields, reversible translation panes, service-continuity diagrams, environmental controls, access histories, lacunae, and myth-versus-evidence labels—no Library of Alexandria replica, scroll spectacle, pharaonic temple, royal portrait hall, or Greek costume.<br>
**Institutional role:** A conservation, library, translation, research, and public-memory service within existing Law of Integrity, Precog, Nightingale, Chromacord, consent, custody, and Recovery Ward boundaries; it cannot diagnose, clear, seize a collection, or establish doctrine.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only multilingual record comparison, acquisition and custody provenance, translation revision, institution-state and service continuity, access-history display, curriculum, and future bounded conservation references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Separate the 332–323 precursor, 323–305 satrapy, 305–281 kingdom, and Ptolemy II's early reign; attach every item to language, community, maker or scribe, prior custodian, acquisition method, access, source date, conservation state, and uncertainty; do not invent the Library's building, catalog size, universal scope, or public access.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Integrity; Precog; Prima Donna; Persephones; Nightingales; Chromacord; Recovery Ward; see [Glaüshouse canon links](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>

**Confidence:** High for the major political phases, Alexandria's royal centrality, Greek administrative use, and continued Egyptian scripts and institutions; medium for individual early administrative arrangements; low to contested for the Museum and Library's exact founder, foundation date, holdings, acquisition, access, and physical form.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/ancient-egyptian-economy/ptolemaic-period-33230-bce/029A98BAB6F458BA138E6E028C71CAB3); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.ucl.ac.uk/museums-static/digitalegypt/writing/library/alexandria.html); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://www.ucl.ac.uk/museums-static/digitalegypt/writing/library/greek.html); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://www.ucl.ac.uk/museums-static/digitalegypt/writing/demotic.html); [Scholarly or collection source 5](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-hellenic-studies/article/power-of-statues-constructing-imperial-narratives-under-the-ptolemies/37C9F0FF0F989B888127DAD99E32980E)<br>

## Regional Witness Conservatory

**Manifestation ID:** `manifestation.glaushouse.regional_witness_conservatory`<br>
**Manifestation:** Regional Witness Conservatory<br>
**Primary House:** [Glaüshouse](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Preserve locally distinct sites, material traditions, mobile histories, language witnesses, copies, translations, and institutional changes without diagnosing them as one ethnic or political system.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Dispersed early Aramaean-labeled communities, locally shaped Syrian and upper Mesopotamian city-states, a wider regional cultural koine, Assyrian external descriptions, and sparse Old Aramaic evidence emerging near the Point boundary. See the [Aram dossier](dossiers/age_02_point_01/ARAM.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.glaushouse.aram.multi_center_city_state_formation`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.aram.multi_center_city_state_formation), [`gift.glaushouse.aram.regional_cultural_exchange_with_local_variation`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.aram.regional_cultural_exchange_with_local_variation), [`gift.glaushouse.aram.early_aramaic_language_and_inscriptional_continuity`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.aram.early_aramaic_language_and_inscriptional_continuity)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Glaüshouse receives distributed custody, local variation, comparative preservation, and honest language chronology. It does not receive one Aram state, a uniform Aramaean material culture, a later imperial lingua franca backdated to 1200, or clinical Clearance from classification.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A modern network of small glass, ceramic, planted, and dark-blue study rooms connected by a shared comparison interface, with site-specific material tables, language-status bands, movable regional maps, visible custody trails, and empty space for missing evidence; no Damascus court replica, generic caravan décor, or invented Aramaean motifs.<br>
**Institutional role:** A distributed library, conservation, and translation program within existing Glaüshouse Precog, Nightingale, custody, access, and Integrity boundaries.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only site comparison, terminology and language-status metadata, copy and translation lineage, artifact custody, uncertainty display, curriculum, and future bounded preservation references.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Store local polity and site before umbrella identity; label Assyrian ethnonyms as external and adversarial where appropriate; keep spoken-language inference, earliest inscriptions, later standardization, and imperial Aramaic in separate periods.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Integrity; Precog; Prima Donna; Persephones; Nightingales; Chromacord; Recovery Ward; see [Glaüshouse canon links](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** regional witness conservator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** site-by-site evidence comparison, language-status metadata, custody history, and curriculum references<br>
**Confidence:** High that Aram is an uneven external and regional label and that material culture does not define one early identity; medium for reconstructing tenth-century polity formation; low for assigning sparse early inscriptions to a uniform language standard.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://academic.oup.com/reference/62341/reference-article-abstract/554094159); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.degruyterbrill.com/document/doi/10.18574/nyu/9781479834648.003.0021/html); [Scholarly or collection source 3](https://resources.metmuseum.org/resources/metpublications/pdf/Assyria_to_Iberia_at_the_Dawn_of_the_Classical_Age.pdf); [Scholarly or collection source 4](https://www.degruyterbrill.com/serial/sari-b/html)<br>
**Status:** Reviewed flagship; design remains Working until separately adopted<br>

**Manifestation:** Comnenian Continuity and Rupture Conservatory<br>
**Primary House:** [Glaüshouse](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md)<br>
**Purpose:** Separate law, city, archive, frontier, object, treaty, damage, and successor custody through Comnenian recovery and 1204.<br>
**Historical ancestry:** Comnenian law and administration, Constantinople, churches, manuscripts, fortifications, diplomacy, Crusader contact, and successor states after 1204. See the [Byzantine Empire dossier](dossiers/age_03_point_05/BYZANTINE_EMPIRE_COMNENIAN_CONTINUITY_AND_1204_1050_1204.md).<br>
**Source Gifts:** [`gift.glaushouse.byzantine_empire.legal_and_administrative_continuity`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.byzantine_empire.legal_and_administrative_continuity), [`gift.glaushouse.byzantine_empire.diplomatic_and_frontier_recovery`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.byzantine_empire.diplomatic_and_frontier_recovery), [`gift.glaushouse.byzantine_empire.archive_and_urban_maintenance`](ANCESTRAL_GIFTS_REGISTRY.md#gift.glaushouse.byzantine_empire.archive_and_urban_maintenance)<br>
**Modern House interpretation:** Glaüshouse receives conservation and continuity evidence, not imperial restoration, doctrine, or Clearance.<br>
**Physical appearance:** A contemporary dark-blue, glass, ceramic, and planted conservatory with law and archive stations, city-service diagrams, object condition, frontier and treaty layers, damage and successor custody fields; no palace or shrine replica.<br>
**Institutional role:** A conservation, library, accessibility, and recovery program within existing Glaüshouse authority.<br>
**Gameplay potential:** Read-only law and archive provenance, city and frontier service, treaty, damage, restoration, successor custody, and uncertainty.<br>
**Implementation notes:** Separate Alexios I, John II, Manuel I, 1176, 1185, and 1204; distinguish Latin occupation from Byzantine successor continuity.<br>
**Current canon connection:** Law of Integrity; Precog; Prima Donna; Persephones; Nightingales; Chromacord; Recovery Ward; see [Glaüshouse canon links](houses/GLAUSHOUSE_ANCESTRY.md#existing-canon-bridge).<br>
**Possible NPC role:** Comnenian-continuity curator<br>
**Possible Hueman data hook:** law and archive provenance, city, frontier, treaty, damage, restoration, and successor custody<br>
**Confidence:** High for major rulers, 1204 rupture, and institutional continuity; variable for local service and object custody.<br>
**Sources:** [Scholarly or collection source 1](https://www.britannica.com/place/Byzantine-Empire); [Scholarly or collection source 2](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/new-cambridge-medieval-history/)<br>
**Status:** Reviewed flagship; design remains Working until separately adopted<br>
